## gold:t1_1kgs13:mjwk:open

Who came from Judah by Yahweh's word?


**Run 1: KEEP**

- Content / target_preservation: not_applicable — No original item or explicit intended target is supplied.
- Content / question_support: pass — 13:1 states that a man of God came from Judah by the word of the LORD; 'Yahweh' functions as the question's naming equivalent.
- Content / answer_support: pass — 13:1 explicitly identifies the arriving person as 'a man of God.'
- Content / answer_uniqueness: pass — Only the man of God is described as coming from Judah by the LORD's word.
- Content / context_sufficiency: pass — 13:1 contains the person's designation, origin, and authorization.
- Content / answer_leakage: pass — The question gives the arrival circumstances without naming the person.
- Content / mcq_validity: not_applicable — This is an open question.
- Language / naturalness: 1/3 — 'By Yahweh's word' is a somewhat stiff biblical expression for authorization or direction. The rest of 'Who came from Judah' is direct.
- Language / processing: 1/3 — Readers must interpret 'by Yahweh's word' as describing the authority or direction behind the coming, rather than its physical manner. This creates a modest interpretive demand, but the identity-question structure remains simple.
- Language / context_economy: 0/3 — 'From Judah' and 'by Yahweh's word' specify the arrival being queried without adding an unrelated setup.

**Run 2: REWRITE**

- Content / target_preservation: not_applicable — No original item or explicit intended target is supplied.
- Content / question_support: pass — 13:1 states that a man of God came from Judah by the word of the LORD.
- Content / answer_support: pass — The expected answer directly repeats the passage's designation of the person.
- Content / answer_uniqueness: pass — No other person is described as making this arrival.
- Content / context_sufficiency: pass — The arrival and all identifying details requested by the question occur in 13:1.
- Content / answer_leakage: pass — The question does not supply the designation 'a man of God.'
- Content / mcq_validity: not_applicable — This is an open question.
- Language / naturalness: 2/3 — “Who came from Judah by Yahweh's word?” uses the nonconversational expression “by Yahweh's word,” which requires readers to recover the intended sense of divine direction.
- Language / processing: 1/3 — The attachment of “by Yahweh's word” to “came” requires an interpretive step about what motivated or directed the journey. The rest of the question is readily parsed.
- Language / context_economy: 0/3 — “From Judah” and “by Yahweh's word” are compact identifying details about the journey, not unnecessary setup.

## gold:t1_1kgs13:mjwk:mcq

Who comes from Judah by Yahweh's word?

A. A prophet from Bethel
B. King Jeroboam
C. A priest of the high places
D. A man of God
E. I can't tell from this passage

**Run 1: KEEP**

- Content / target_preservation: pass — The final question preserves the original person's origin, divine authorization, and requested identity.
- Content / question_support: pass — Verse 1 says a man of God came from Judah by the word of the LORD; 'Yahweh' functions as the corresponding divine-name rendering.
- Content / answer_support: pass — The arriving person is explicitly called 'a man of God.'
- Content / answer_uniqueness: pass — Only the man of God is described as arriving from Judah.
- Content / context_sufficiency: pass — The person's description, origin, destination, and authorization are all in verse 1.
- Content / answer_leakage: pass — The origin and authorization anchor the event without supplying the person's designation.
- Content / mcq_validity: pass — D is explicit. A changes the origin to Bethel; B is already beside the altar; C is not identified as the arrival. E is unwarranted.
- Language / naturalness: 1/3 — “Who comes from Judah” is direct, but “by Yahweh's word” is compressed, biblical-register phrasing rather than ordinary conversational English.
- Language / processing: 1/3 — Readers interpret “by Yahweh's word” as the authority or prompting for the coming, rather than as a manner of travel. This requires a modest interpretive step, but the short question and simple identity options limit the demand.
- Language / context_economy: 0/3 — “From Judah” and “by Yahweh's word” characterize the arrival being asked about. They do not form an excessive introductory frame.

**Run 2: REWRITE**

- Content / target_preservation: pass — The present-tense retelling preserves the original arrival event, origin, divine authorization, and person to be identified.
- Content / question_support: pass — 13:1 explicitly describes a man of God coming from Judah to Bethel by the word of the LORD.
- Content / answer_support: pass — 'A man of God' is the passage's designation for the arriving person.
- Content / answer_uniqueness: pass — Only the man of God is described as arriving from Judah by the LORD's word.
- Content / context_sufficiency: pass — 13:1 supplies the person, origin, destination, and authorization in one sentence.
- Content / answer_leakage: pass — The question gives the identifying circumstances but not the person's designation.
- Content / mcq_validity: pass — D is explicit. A gives a different origin; B is already beside the altar; C is not identified as the arriving person. E conflicts with the explicit answer.
- Language / naturalness: 2/3 — “By Yahweh's word” in “Who comes from Judah by Yahweh's word?” is not ordinary contemporary phrasing for acting under divine direction and requires interpretation.
- Language / processing: 1/3 — Readers must interpret “by Yahweh's word” as the direction behind the journey rather than a literal means of travel. This avoidable interpretive step is localized in an otherwise short question.
- Language / context_economy: 0/3 — “From Judah” and “by Yahweh's word” specify the origin and divine direction of the journey; neither is extraneous framing.

## gold:t1_1kgs13:kcyl:open

What happens when the man of God prays?


**Run 1: KEEP**

- Content / target_preservation: not_applicable — No original item or explicit intended target is supplied.
- Content / question_support: pass — Verse 6 states that the man of God interceded with the LORD and the king's hand was restored.
- Content / answer_support: pass — The passage explicitly reports that 'the king's hand was restored to him as it was before.'
- Content / answer_uniqueness: pass — Restoration of the hand is the only outcome reported following the intercession.
- Content / context_sufficiency: pass — The passage supplies the withered hand, the king's request, the intercession, and its outcome.
- Content / answer_leakage: pass — The question does not state the outcome. The king's request within the passage does not itself establish that restoration occurred.
- Content / mcq_validity: not_applicable — This is an open question.
- Language / naturalness: 0/3 — "What happens when the man of God prays?" is a direct, idiomatic outcome question.
- Language / processing: 0/3 — "When the man of God prays" provides a readily parsed event anchor for "What happens," with no competing roles or references within the question.
- Language / context_economy: 0/3 — The prayer clause is useful for identifying the event whose outcome is requested; there is no unnecessary framing.

**Run 2: KEEP**

- Content / target_preservation: not_applicable — No original item or explicit intended target is supplied.
- Content / question_support: pass — Verse 13:6 reports the man of God's intercession and the resulting restoration.
- Content / answer_support: pass — The passage states that 'the king's hand was restored to him as it was before.'
- Content / answer_uniqueness: pass — Only the restoration of the hand is reported as the outcome of this intercession.
- Content / context_sufficiency: pass — The damaged hand, request for prayer, intercession, and restoration are all supplied.
- Content / answer_leakage: pass — The question mentions prayer without revealing its outcome.
- Content / mcq_validity: not_applicable — This is an open question.
- Language / naturalness: 0/3 — “What happens when the man of God prays?” is a direct, idiomatic outcome question.
- Language / processing: 0/3 — “when the man of God prays” identifies one event, and “What happens” requests its outcome. There are no competing pronouns or embedded viewpoints to untangle.
- Language / context_economy: 0/3 — The short clause “when the man of God prays” supplies the event needed to focus the question, with no excess setup.

## gold:t1_1kgs13:kcyl:mcq

What happens when the man of God prays?

A. The old prophet was healed
B. The lion was tamed
C. The altar was rebuilt
D. The king’s hand was restored
E. I can't tell from this passage

**Run 1: KEEP**

- Content / target_preservation: pass — The final question and answer preserve the original prayer-and-restoration target.
- Content / question_support: pass — Verse 13:6 reports the man of God's intercession and the subsequent restoration of the king's hand.
- Content / answer_support: pass — The passage explicitly says that the king's hand was restored as it was before.
- Content / answer_uniqueness: pass — The restoration is the only outcome attached to the intercession; the altar split earlier.
- Content / context_sufficiency: pass — The passage identifies the injured king, his request, the intercession, and its outcome.
- Content / answer_leakage: pass — The question mentions prayer but does not reveal its outcome.
- Content / mcq_validity: pass — D is explicitly supported. No old prophet is healed, no lion is tamed, and the altar is not rebuilt; E is therefore indefensible.
- Structure / role_tracking: ordinary — What happens when the man of God prays?: The intercessor–beneficiary distinction is part of the intended event relationship, not avoidable structural elaboration.
- Language / naturalness: 0/3 — “What happens when the man of God prays?” is direct and idiomatic. The options, including “The king’s hand was restored,” use ordinary sentence forms.
- Language / processing: 1/3 — Readers distinguish the person praying in “the man of God prays” from the affected person in “The king’s hand was restored.” This is ordinary event-role tracking.
- Language / context_economy: 0/3 — The clause “when the man of God prays” identifies the event being asked about; no unnecessary framing is added.

**Run 2: KEEP**

- Content / target_preservation: pass — The final question preserves the original prayer event and its expected outcome.
- Content / question_support: pass — 13:6 states that the man of God interceded and the king's hand was restored.
- Content / answer_support: pass — The expected answer repeats the outcome explicitly reported after the intercession.
- Content / answer_uniqueness: pass — Restoration of the king's hand is the only reported outcome of this prayer.
- Content / context_sufficiency: pass — The passage supplies one prayer by the man of God and identifies the king's injured hand.
- Content / answer_leakage: pass — The question mentions prayer but does not state its result.
- Content / mcq_validity: pass — D is explicit. A and B concern events not described; C contradicts the reported splitting of the altar rather than rebuilding; E is not defensible.
- Structure / role_tracking: ordinary — What happens when the man of God prays?: Connecting one person's prayer with another person's restoration is central to the target.
- Language / naturalness: 0/3 — “What happens when the man of God prays?” is idiomatic. Options such as “The king’s hand was restored” are naturally formed passive clauses.
- Language / processing: 1/3 — Readers connect the prayer in “when the man of God prays” to an outcome involving another participant. The short options make this ordinary event-and-participant tracking manageable.
- Language / context_economy: 0/3 — “When the man of God prays” supplies the event needed to locate the requested outcome; no additional framing is included.

- Language / context_economy: 0/3 — Naming both "the king" and "the man of God" usefully distinguishes the requested speech from the reply, without excess framing.

- Content / mcq_validity: not_applicable — This is an open question.
- Structure / role_tracking: ordinary — What does the king say to the man of God?: The explicit speaker and recipient distinguish the requested turn without added structural complexity.

- Language / processing: 0/3 — “The king say to the man of God” explicitly marks the speaker and addressee. Readers can identify the requested speech turn without holding nested reports or shifting roles in mind.
- Language / context_economy: 0/3 — The question includes only the speaker, recipient, and request for speech content; “the man of God” identifies the recipient rather than adding background.
